u
. Denote
G_{u}=\delta_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=0.0pt,depth=1.22917pt\picture(3.2,2.6)(-1.6,-1.0)\roundcap\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1,-1)(-1,-.5)(-1,0)\Line(-1,0)(-1,.5)\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1,-1)(1,-.5)(1,0)\Line(1,0)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1,.5)(-1,2)(1,2)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1.1,-1.1)(-.7,-1.9)(-.3,-1.5)\qbezier{}{-0.3}{-1.5}{0}{-1.2}{0.3}{-1.5}\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1.1,-1.1)(.7,-1.9)(.3,-1.5)\put(-0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\put(0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\endpicture}{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=0.0pt,depth=1.22917pt\picture(3.2,2.6)(-1.6,-1.0)\roundcap\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1,-1)(-1,-.5)(-1,0)\Line(-1,0)(-1,.5)\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1,-1)(1,-.5)(1,0)\Line(1,0)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1,.5)(-1,2)(1,2)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1.1,-1.1)(-.7,-1.9)(-.3,-1.5)\qbezier{}{-0.3}{-1.5}{0}{-1.2}{0.3}{-1.5}\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1.1,-1.1)(.7,-1.9)(.3,-1.5)\put(-0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\put(0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\endpicture}{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=0.0pt,depth=1.22917pt\picture(3.2,2.6)(-1.6,-1.0)\roundcap\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1,-1)(-1,-.5)(-1,0)\Line(-1,0)(-1,.5)\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1,-1)(1,-.5)(1,0)\Line(1,0)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1,.5)(-1,2)(1,2)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1.1,-1.1)(-.7,-1.9)(-.3,-1.5)\qbezier{}{-0.3}{-1.5}{0}{-1.2}{0.3}{-1.5}\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1.1,-1.1)(.7,-1.9)(.3,-1.5)\put(-0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\put(0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\endpicture}{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=0.0pt,depth=1.22917pt\picture(3.2,2.6)(-1.6,-1.0)\roundcap\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1,-1)(-1,-.5)(-1,0)\Line(-1,0)(-1,.5)\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1,-1)(1,-.5)(1,0)\Line(1,0)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1,.5)(-1,2)(1,2)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1.1,-1.1)(-.7,-1.9)(-.3,-1.5)\qbezier{}{-0.3}{-1.5}{0}{-1.2}{0.3}{-1.5}\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1.1,-1.1)(.7,-1.9)(.3,-1.5)\put(-0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\put(0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\endpicture}}}(s_{0},u)\setminus B
.

By
Lemma

18.11
we have that
(S^{\prime\prime},x^{\prime})=\mathsf{bshift}[{(\delta_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=0.0pt,depth=1.22917pt\picture(3.2,2.6)(-1.6,-1.0)\roundcap\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1,-1)(-1,-.5)(-1,0)\Line(-1,0)(-1,.5)\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1,-1)(1,-.5)(1,0)\Line(1,0)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1,.5)(-1,2)(1,2)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1.1,-1.1)(-.7,-1.9)(-.3,-1.5)\qbezier{}{-0.3}{-1.5}{0}{-1.2}{0.3}{-1.5}\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1.1,-1.1)(.7,-1.9)(.3,-1.5)\put(-0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\put(0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\endpicture}{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=0.0pt,depth=1.22917pt\picture(3.2,2.6)(-1.6,-1.0)\roundcap\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1,-1)(-1,-.5)(-1,0)\Line(-1,0)(-1,.5)\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1,-1)(1,-.5)(1,0)\Line(1,0)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1,.5)(-1,2)(1,2)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1.1,-1.1)(-.7,-1.9)(-.3,-1.5)\qbezier{}{-0.3}{-1.5}{0}{-1.2}{0.3}{-1.5}\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1.1,-1.1)(.7,-1.9)(.3,-1.5)\put(-0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\put(0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\endpicture}{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=0.0pt,depth=1.22917pt\picture(3.2,2.6)(-1.6,-1.0)\roundcap\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1,-1)(-1,-.5)(-1,0)\Line(-1,0)(-1,.5)\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1,-1)(1,-.5)(1,0)\Line(1,0)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1,.5)(-1,2)(1,2)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1.1,-1.1)(-.7,-1.9)(-.3,-1.5)\qbezier{}{-0.3}{-1.5}{0}{-1.2}{0.3}{-1.5}\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1.1,-1.1)(.7,-1.9)(.3,-1.5)\put(-0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\put(0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\endpicture}{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=0.0pt,depth=1.22917pt\picture(3.2,2.6)(-1.6,-1.0)\roundcap\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1,-1)(-1,-.5)(-1,0)\Line(-1,0)(-1,.5)\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1,-1)(1,-.5)(1,0)\Line(1,0)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1,.5)(-1,2)(1,2)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1.1,-1.1)(-.7,-1.9)(-.3,-1.5)\qbezier{}{-0.3}{-1.5}{0}{-1.2}{0.3}{-1.5}\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1.1,-1.1)(.7,-1.9)(.3,-1.5)\put(-0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\put(0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\endpicture}}}(s_{0},u),x^{k})}/{\rho}]
is a stable cycle, and that for every
s\in B
we have

\mathsf{mwt}(\mathsf{bshift}[{s}/{\rho}]\xrightarrow{\alpha_{S^{\prime\prime},x^{\prime}}}S)=\infty
. Indeed, this follows since the SSRI is positive and
B=V_{1}\cup\ldots\cup V_{\ell}
(i.e.,
\ell^{\prime}=\ell
in the notation of
Lemma

18.11
). Denote
B^{\prime}=\mathsf{bshift}[{B}/{\rho}]
.

By
Definition

18.1
we have
|x|\leq\frac{1}{{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{m}}}}L_{\mathsf{simp}}(0pt(x)+1)
and since
k\leq|S|\leq{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{m}}}
we have
|x^{\prime}|=|x^{k}|\leq L_{\mathsf{simp}}(0pt(x)+1)
. In addition, we have
0pt(x^{\prime})=0pt(x)
and since
x\in{{\Upsilon^{0}_{\mathsf{simp},0}}}^{*}
then due to the baseline shift,
x^{\prime}
may contain rebase letters, and therefore
x^{\prime}\in{{\Upsilon^{1}_{\mathsf{simp},0}}}
. Thus,
\alpha_{S^{\prime\prime},x^{\prime}}\in{{\Upsilon^{1}_{\mathsf{simp},1}}}
.

We now unfold the prefix
u
as follows: consider the word
u^{\prime}=\mathsf{flatten}(u\wr{\mathsf{max\_eff}(w)}\cdot 4{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{m}}})
, and denote
\boldsymbol{c_{u}^{\prime}}=\mathsf{xconf}(s_{0},u^{\prime})
. Then
G_{u}\cup B=\mathsf{supp}(\boldsymbol{c_{u}^{\prime}})
and by
Lemma

14.15
for every
s^{\prime}\in B
and
s^{\prime\prime}\in G_{u}
we have
\boldsymbol{c_{u}^{\prime}}(s^{\prime\prime})-\boldsymbol{c_{u}^{\prime}}(s^{\prime})>{\mathsf{max\_eff}(w)}\cdot 2{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{m}}}
. Put simply – all states in
G_{u}
are much higher than those in
B
.
We now employ
Corollary

17.5
with two possible outcomes:
if
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{\infty}^{\infty}
has a type-0 witness, we conclude the proof.
Otherwise, we have that
\upphi(u)=\upphi(u^{\prime})
.
This, in turn, means that every
s^{\prime\prime}\in G_{u}
is not dominant at
\boldsymbol{c_{u}^{\prime}}
. Indeed, we have
\upphi(u)\leq{\mathsf{max\_eff}(u)}
, but
\boldsymbol{c_{u}^{\prime}}(s^{\prime\prime})>{\mathsf{max\_eff}(w)}\cdot 2{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{m}}}
, i.e., is far too high.

We now reach a contradiction to the above by finding a dominant state in
G_{u}
, as follows.
Consider the word
u^{\prime}\cdot\mathsf{jump}_{s_{b}\to s}\cdot\alpha_{S^{\prime\prime},x^{\prime}}
. Observe that

\mathsf{wt}(s_{0}\xrightarrow{u^{\prime}}s\xrightarrow{\mathsf{jump}_{s_{b}\to s}}t\xrightarrow{\alpha_{S^{\prime\prime},x^{\prime}}}t)<\infty
where
t
is the baseline-shifted version of
s
on
\rho
(i.e.,
\mathsf{bshift}[{\rho}/{\rho}]:t\xrightarrow{x^{\prime}}t
). Indeed,
s
is reachable from
s_{0}
on the flattened
u^{\prime}
, then the jump letter has weight
0
, and
\mathsf{bshift}[{\rho}/{\rho}]
yields the transition
t\xrightarrow{\alpha_{S^{\prime\prime},x^{\prime}}}t
.
Recall, however, that
s\in G_{u}
and that there are no transitions from
B^{\prime}
on
\alpha_{S^{\prime\prime},x^{\prime}}
. This means that there is no transition from
B
on
\mathsf{jump}_{s_{b}\to s}\cdot\alpha_{S^{\prime\prime},x^{\prime}}
(since the jump letter exactly takes us from
B
to
B^{\prime}
).
We conclude that either
s
, or some lower state in
G_{u}
, is dominant, which is a contradiction.
∎

18.4
GSRI – A Toolbox

We continue by developing some GSRI-specific tools.

We start by observing that for GSRI, the minimal set in the partition (namely
V_{1}
, whose in particular responsible for
\uppsi
), does not generate negative cycles on
x
. This can be viewed as a counterpart of
Proposition

18.6
.

Proposition 18.15
.

Consider a GSRI
uxyv
.
In the notations of
Definition

18.1
, for every
r\in V_{1}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
we have
\mathsf{mwt}(r\xrightarrow{x^{k}}r)\geq 0
. In particular
k_{1,x}\geq 0
.

Proof.

Assume by way of contradiction there is some
r\in V_{1}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mathsf{mwt}(r\xrightarrow{x^{k}}r)<0
.
By the pigeonhole principle there is some
k^{\prime}\leq|S|
such that
\mathsf{mwt}(r\xrightarrow{x^{k^{\prime}}}r)<0
.
However, observe that in a GSRI, the minimal runs after
u
and after
ux
end in a state in
V_{1}
. Since
\uppsi(u)\geq\uppsi(ux)
we have
k_{1,x}\geq 0
. Therefore, we have

\mathsf{xconf}(\boldsymbol{c_{u}},x^{k^{\prime}})(r)=\boldsymbol{c_{u}}+k^{\prime}k_{1,x}\geq\boldsymbol{c_{u}}
, so
\mathsf{mwt}(r\xrightarrow{x^{k^{\prime}}}r)\geq 0
, in contradiction.
∎

The next lemma is the central property of GSRI, and is the counterpart of
Lemma

18.14
. Conceptually, it is what links the charge to the potential.

Lemma 18.16

(From GSRI to Stable GSRI or High Potential)
.

Consider a GSRI
w=uxyv
, then either
(\delta_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=0.0pt,depth=1.22917pt\picture(3.2,2.6)(-1.6,-1.0)\roundcap\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1,-1)(-1,-.5)(-1,0)\Line(-1,0)(-1,.5)\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1,-1)(1,-.5)(1,0)\Line(1,0)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1,.5)(-1,2)(1,2)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1.1,-1.1)(-.7,-1.9)(-.3,-1.5)\qbezier{}{-0.3}{-1.5}{0}{-1.2}{0.3}{-1.5}\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1.1,-1.1)(.7,-1.9)(.3,-1.5)\put(-0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\put(0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\endpicture}{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=0.0pt,depth=1.22917pt\picture(3.2,2.6)(-1.6,-1.0)\roundcap\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1,-1)(-1,-.5)(-1,0)\Line(-1,0)(-1,.5)\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1,-1)(1,-.5)(1,0)\Line(1,0)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1,.5)(-1,2)(1,2)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1.1,-1.1)(-.7,-1.9)(-.3,-1.5)\qbezier{}{-0.3}{-1.5}{0}{-1.2}{0.3}{-1.5}\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1.1,-1.1)(.7,-1.9)(.3,-1.5)\put(-0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\put(0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\endpicture}{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=0.0pt,depth=1.22917pt\picture(3.2,2.6)(-1.6,-1.0)\roundcap\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1,-1)(-1,-.5)(-1,0)\Line(-1,0)(-1,.5)\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1,-1)(1,-.5)(1,0)\Line(1,0)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1,.5)(-1,2)(1,2)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1.1,-1.1)(-.7,-1.9)(-.3,-1.5)\qbezier{}{-0.3}{-1.5}{0}{-1.2}{0.3}{-1.5}\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1.1,-1.1)(.7,-1.9)(.3,-1.5)\put(-0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\put(0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\endpicture}{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=0.0pt,depth=1.22917pt\picture(3.2,2.6)(-1.6,-1.0)\roundcap\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1,-1)(-1,-.5)(-1,0)\Line(-1,0)(-1,.5)\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1,-1)(1,-.5)(1,0)\Line(1,0)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1,.5)(-1,2)(1,2)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1.1,-1.1)(-.7,-1.9)(-.3,-1.5)\qbezier{}{-0.3}{-1.5}{0}{-1.2}{0.3}{-1.5}\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1.1,-1.1)(.7,-1.9)(.3,-1.5)\put(-0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\put(0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\endpicture}}}(s_{0},u),x)
is a stable cycle or there is a word

such that
\upphi(w^{\prime})\geq\textbf{H}
.

Proof.

We start with an intuitive overview: if
(\delta_{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=0.0pt,depth=1.22917pt\picture(3.2,2.6)(-1.6,-1.0)\roundcap\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1,-1)(-1,-.5)(-1,0)\Line(-1,0)(-1,.5)\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1,-1)(1,-.5)(1,0)\Line(1,0)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1,.5)(-1,2)(1,2)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1.1,-1.1)(-.7,-1.9)(-.3,-1.5)\qbezier{}{-0.3}{-1.5}{0}{-1.2}{0.3}{-1.5}\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1.1,-1.1)(.7,-1.9)(.3,-1.5)\put(-0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\put(0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\endpicture}{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=0.0pt,depth=1.22917pt\picture(3.2,2.6)(-1.6,-1.0)\roundcap\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1,-1)(-1,-.5)(-1,0)\Line(-1,0)(-1,.5)\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1,-1)(1,-.5)(1,0)\Line(1,0)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1,.5)(-1,2)(1,2)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1.1,-1.1)(-.7,-1.9)(-.3,-1.5)\qbezier{}{-0.3}{-1.5}{0}{-1.2}{0.3}{-1.5}\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1.1,-1.1)(.7,-1.9)(.3,-1.5)\put(-0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\put(0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\endpicture}{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=0.0pt,depth=1.22917pt\picture(3.2,2.6)(-1.6,-1.0)\roundcap\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1,-1)(-1,-.5)(-1,0)\Line(-1,0)(-1,.5)\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1,-1)(1,-.5)(1,0)\Line(1,0)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1,.5)(-1,2)(1,2)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1.1,-1.1)(-.7,-1.9)(-.3,-1.5)\qbezier{}{-0.3}{-1.5}{0}{-1.2}{0.3}{-1.5}\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1.1,-1.1)(.7,-1.9)(.3,-1.5)\put(-0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\put(0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\endpicture}{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=0.0pt,depth=1.22917pt\picture(3.2,2.6)(-1.6,-1.0)\roundcap\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1,-1)(-1,-.5)(-1,0)\Line(-1,0)(-1,.5)\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1,-1)(1,-.5)(1,0)\Line(1,0)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1,.5)(-1,2)(1,2)(1,.5)\cbezier(-1.5,-1.5)(-1.1,-1.1)(-.7,-1.9)(-.3,-1.5)\qbezier{}{-0.3}{-1.5}{0}{-1.2}{0.3}{-1.5}\cbezier(1.5,-1.5)(1.1,-1.1)(.7,-1.9)(.3,-1.5)\put(-0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\put(0.4,0.7){\circle*{0.4}}\endpicture}}}(s_{0},u),x)
is not a stable cycle, then there is some minimal negative-slope run
\rho
on some
x^{k}
. By
Proposition

18.15
this cycle cannot stem from a
V_{1}
state, so it stems from a higher (possibly ghost) state.
By
Lemma

18.11
we can shift on
\rho
to obtain a cactus letter
\alpha_{S^{\prime\prime},x^{\prime}}
that has no finite weight transition from
V_{1}
.

By the gap property (
Item

3a
in
Definition

18.1
) this state is
much
higher than
V_{1}
.
We flatten
u
to a word
u^{\prime}
such that the ghost states are also much higher than
H
above
V_{1}
.

Using a jump letter to
S^{\prime\prime}
and
\alpha_{S^{\prime\prime},x^{\prime}}
as a suffix, we get that the maximal dominant state after reading
u^{\prime}
is not in
V_{1}
, and is therefore more than
H
above it. Finally, we can shift so that the baseline run is in
V_{1}
, and we get potential greater than
H
.

Figure 20
:
Proof outline of

. We start with the negative run
\rho
and baseline-shift on it. This casuses

to jump to
\infty
(by
Lemma

18.11
. The GSRI gaps are larger than
H
, so by baseline-shifting again to a minimal run to
V_{1}
and using a jump letter, we obtain a high-potential configuration.

Assume that

is not a stable cycle (otherwise we are done). Therefore, by

there is some

).
